  Cerebras missed on core revenue ($180M vs $194M LSEG consensus, ~7% short) but beat on loss (-$0.05 adj vs -$0.17), guided Q3 core revenue to $214–216M and raised FY core to $880–890M from $855–865M (+~$25M, ~3%), with core gross margin guided to 38–40% and RPO of $25.4B; net loss was $450.5M, of which $386.6M is stock comp. Stock -14% after hours despite the raise and management's 'demand through the roof' framing plus a stated intent to triple revenue next fiscal year — that is the sell-the-beat reflex the investor is watching into the Jul 28–31 hyperscaler window, and it is the most transferable read here: AI names are now being marked on the quality/margin of growth, not the growth itself. Two coverage-specific facts: the AMD partnership goes into production later this year (mild positive for the amd-parity thesis that AMD is winning inference-adjacent attach, though no dollar size disclosed), and OpenAI is serving GPT-5.6-Sol on Cerebras, evidence that frontier labs are multi-sourcing low-latency inference away from NVDA silicon. But 38–40% core GM on 'premium-priced' fast inference is roughly half Nvidia's structural level, which suggests wafer-scale economics still subsidize the latency win — refuted if GM tracks above 40% next quarter on scale/component pricing as Feldman argues; confirmed if the $25.4B RPO converts at flat-to-lower margin. Cloud revenue of $126M of $180M core also means the business is increasingly a capacity operator, so its buildout is incremental demand for TSM and power/cooling names (VRT), not a threat to them.

- `5` **BiCS9 QLC: Kioxias Architektur-Mix gibt es auch mit 4 Bit pro Zelle - ComputerBase** → [[Theses/285A - Kioxia]]
  Kioxia detailed BiCS9 QLC at 2Tbit (256GB) per die on 230 layers with a 4.8Gb/s interface (+33% vs BiCS8's 3.6Gb/s) and a 6-plane architecture for higher read/write bandwidth and better power efficiency; BiCS9 is explicitly a hybrid — BiCS8 cells bonded to a newer BiCS10-class CMOS I/O via CBA wafer bonding — which Kioxia says keeps 'investment costs relatively low', while 332-layer BiCS10 (37.6 Gbit/mm² QLC) is reserved for enterprise. No mass-production date was given, which is the gap in the story. For SNDK (co-development/manufacturing partner) this is a cost-curve item: density stays near BiCS8 but bit cost and interface speed improve without a full new-cell capex cycle, implying better consumer NAND margins in a tightening pricing environment without adding oversupply risk (nand-cycle). The 4.8Gb/s I/O plus CBA bonding is also the enabling stack for 'high bandwidth flash' concepts (hbf-flash) and keeps wafer/hybrid-bonding tool demand in play (BESI, ASMI); confirm with a stated mass-production quarter and any HBF product announcement using the BiCS9/10 I/O.

  Aixtron swung to a Q2 2026 net profit of €19.15m from a €21.9m Q1 loss, with optoelectronics now ~54% of equipment revenue versus power electronics (GaN/SiC) collapsing to ~22% of H1 equipment sales from 71% in H1 2025 — a mix inversion driven by AI-datacenter optical interconnect and laser MOCVD demand. H1 revenue still fell to €174.5m from €249.9m, but backlog stood at €456.9m at June 30 and management reaffirmed FY26 guidance of €560m ±30m revenue, 17–20% EBIT margin and ~42% gross margin, with Q3 guided to €180m ±20m; €40m is going into a new Penang, Malaysia site where groundworks have started. This is an inflection-grade datapoint for the photonics/CPO and inp-capacity questions: MOCVD tool demand for laser/InP epitaxy is the upstream tell for optical component capacity landing in 2027–28, a read-across to IQE (epiwafer), LITE and AAOI (component supply), and to CPO adoption timing at the switch level. The stock is +151.8% YTD and ~30% below the €62.68 June high, so the risk is timing, not direction — confirmation is on-schedule Q4 system deliveries and continued optoelectronics order share; refutal would be a backlog push-out or FY guidance cut at Q3.
  Sources: [www.boerse-express.com](https://www.boerse-express.com/news/articles/aixtron-aktie-nettogewinn-von-1915-millionen-euro-938016)
- `5` **NVIDIA Plans $2 Billion Investment in ASPEED Technology (NVDA)** → [[Theses/NVDA - Nvidia]]
  Nvidia is reported (Aug 11) to be weighing a $2B strategic investment in ASPEED Technology, the dominant supplier of baseboard management controllers for servers. Thin on terms, but the mechanism matters for the rubin-ramp question: BMC is a single-source bottleneck component in every AI server board, and an equity stake would be another instance of Nvidia pre-buying/locking scarce non-GPU supply ahead of the Vera Rubin ramp, alongside its power and infrastructure investments. Inference: if consummated it implies Nvidia sees BMC/board-management supply as a 2027 constraint, which would be a mild negative signal for ODM-side cost and a positive volume signal for the Rubin platform ramp. Confirm via a formal ASPEED filing on the placement size and price. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [www.gurufocus.com](https://www.gurufocus.com/news/9026835/nvidia-plans-2-billion-investment-in-aspeed-technology-nvda)

  Headline claim only: on Aug 10, 2026 Sony announced a collaboration with TSMC to establish a joint venture/investment in Japan's semiconductor market; the excerpt is truncated with no capex figure, node, or site named. If this is an expansion beyond the existing Kumamoto (JASM) structure it matters for the tsmc-capex and tsmc-nodes questions — incremental Japanese capacity is image-sensor/specialty-node weighted, i.e. non-AI capex that competes for TSMC's capital budget rather than adding N2/A16 or CoWoS supply. Confirm/refute with the actual press release: JV ownership split, yen commitment, node (28/22nm vs 12/16nm), and whether TSMC frames it as incremental to or inside prior capex guidance. *(headline only)*

- `5` **CoreWeave Closes $2.6 Billion Loan Facility, Expanding Financing Flexibility for AI Infrastructure**
  CoreWeave closed a $2.6B delayed-draw term loan (DDTL 5.5) that, per the release, broadens the range of customer contracts eligible for publicly syndicated HPC-infrastructure-backed financing versus prior facilities. The structural point beyond the size: widening contract eligibility means lower-rated or non-hyperscaler offtakers can now be collateralized, which expands how much of the $129B backlog is financeable — the binding constraint on neocloud buildouts is capital access, not demand, so this is a direct throughput signal for NVDA/VRT order timing and for IREN/NBIS as comparables that must fund on similar terms. Inference: if syndication markets keep absorbing this paper, the AI capex cycle extends past what hyperscaler capex guides alone imply. Refuting datapoint would be a widened spread or pulled syndication on the next facility, or draw terms disclosed at materially higher cost of capital in the 10-Q. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [businesswire.com](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260810690198/en/CoreWeave-Closes-2.6-Billion-Loan-Facility-Expanding-Financing-Flexibility-for-AI-Infrastructure/)

- `4` **ServiceNow Aktie: Now Assist überschreitet 1-Milliarde-Dollar-Marke** → [[Theses/NOW - ServiceNow]]
  ServiceNow's Now Assist portfolio crossed $1.0B in annual contract value in Q2 — a 9x increase in nine months — which is the clearest hard-dollar evidence yet that agentic AI is converting to contracted revenue rather than pilot spend at a large enterprise software vendor. Supporting Q2 figures: revenue $3.99B (+24%), subscription $3.88B (+25%), renewal rate 98%, adj. EPS $0.90 vs $0.86 consensus, customers >$5M ACV +23% to 658, RPO +21% to $29.0B, and FY26 subscription guidance raised to $15.76–15.78B; Citizens flagged four Q3 federal wins each >$10M, suggesting public-sector traction. The counterweight is that the AI narrative is being funded by headcount: ~300 cuts in Santa Clara/San Diego with up to 1,000 by year-end off ~29,000, plus ~$1.73M of insider selling. For coverage this is the positive-side template for the agentic-attach thesis at INTU, PCOR, IOT and NOW itself — but it also sharpens the INTU bear-watch by proving that agentic ACV can be built fast; refutation of the durability read would be Now Assist ACV growth decelerating sharply once the initial upsell cohort laps.
  Sources: [www.boerse-express.com](https://www.boerse-express.com/news/articles/servicenow-aktie-now-assist-ueberschreitet-1-milliarde-dollar-marke-937770) · [www.boerse-express.com](https://www.boerse-express.com/news/articles/servicenow-aktie-1576-milliarden-jahresprognose-angehoben-937570)
- `4` **CoreWeave's $129 Billion AI Backlog Changes the Bull Case**
  CoreWeave's Q2 beat consensus revenue by only ~80bps at the midpoint — arguably tepid against a high bar — yet the stock and the tape rallied because the disclosed backlog reached $129B, which the source frames as the bull case shifting from near-term revenue to contracted duration. Mechanism: backlog is the cleanest forward demand signal for the compute supply chain (NVDA GPUs, VRT power/thermal, and by extension TSM/CoWoS wafer-out), and it is more informative than a 0.8% revenue beat because it is booked capacity that must be built. What to watch to confirm: the backlog's customer concentration and whether it is take-or-pay, plus the delivery schedule — $129B of obligations against a financing-constrained balance sheet only becomes revenue if debt capacity keeps pace (see the DDTL item), so treat backlog growth and incremental facility closings as a single linked signal, not two. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [marketbeat.com](https://www.marketbeat.com/articles/coreweaves-129-billion-ai-backlog-changes-the-bull-case/)
- `4` **Zuckerberg Plans to Spend $145 Billion on AI This Year. Barclays Says Major Productivity Gains Remain Elusive**
  Meta narrowed FY26 capex to $130–145B while Q2 free cash flow collapsed 91% YoY to $784M, long-term debt reached $83.66B, and EPS of $6.18 missed the $7.22 consensus — breaking a six-quarter beat streak, with shares down 9.7% YTD; Amazon is guided near $220B, and the aggregate US big-tech outlay is put at roughly $800B. The bifurcation is the story for the meta-capex and hyperscaler-guides questions: the spend itself is being reaffirmed (bullish for NVDA, AVGO/custom-XPU, VRT, TSM CoWoS demand), but it is now being funded by debt against near-zero FCF, which is precisely the condition under which a capex guide gets cut mid-cycle rather than raised. Barclays' Millar adds the demand-side caveat — Information sector growth decelerated to 1.5% in Q1 2026 from 2.5% in Q4 2025, and 'industries adopting AI more rapidly do not appear to be experiencing faster growth' — a 1980s-IT-boom lag framing that, if the market adopts it, compresses multiples for the supply chain before it cuts units. This sharpens the sell-the-beat risk into the late-July guide window: confirmation would be any hyperscaler trimming the top of a capex range or extending server depreciation lives; refutation would be another Amazon-style 'still short of capacity' upsize.
  Sources: [247wallst.com](https://247wallst.com/investing/2026/08/11/zuckerberg-plans-to-spend-145-billion-on-ai-this-year-barclays-says-major-productivity-gains-remain-elusive/)
